tbmm-2016-02-29sit02spe0145par0583	Mr. President, members of parliament, I have made a pledge on the Turkish Accreditation Agency's budget for 2016. I salute your committee. Right now, I'm going to inform your people about the Turkish Accreditation Agency. The Turkish Accreditation Agency was established in 1999 with Law 4457 in order to improve quality infrastructure in our country, ensure the credibility of products and services and boost international trade, one of our institutions in the EU integration process. ENGLISH has so far registered 1.146 adaptive assessment agencies. The quality substructure in our country is strengthened, there is confidence in the market, quality products and services are spreading, providing international validity of the goods and services produced in our country. Accreditation is a quality infrastructure created by adaptability assessment agencies to support the credibility and validity of the study and the competence certification documents. A product or service that has an actuation certificate inspires confidence that it provides practical requirements for itself. Because of this systematic activation, Akredition contributes to the removal of technical barriers in trade. The mechanism of confidence among Akreditation agencies is based on multilateral recognition agreements with regional organisations such as the International Accreditation Forum, the International Laboratory Acreditation Union, and the European Accredition Union. Acredity companies are spreading not only to Istanbul or the Marmara Region, but to all the developing and industrial cities of Anatolia. For example, my constituency of the election, Kayseri, still operates as a lab, examination and documentation agency from ÜRKAK, which operates at 26 companies. Turkey's Accreditation Agency, which started serving as an accordion service in 2001, was a full member of the European Accreditation Union in 2002, signing mutual recognition agreements in 2006. Since 2008, he has signed bilateral recognition agreements with the European Accreditation Association in all of the Accreditation Areas on recognition agreements. In 2015, Turkey hosted international organisations such as the Multilateral Agreement Council meeting, the Press and Communication Committee meeting and the Health Work Group meeting, and finally successfully carried out the assessment audit conducted by the European Accordation Agency every four years. TÜRKAK remains a national authority on promoting and good laboratory practices of institutions abroad and abroad. The institution operates not only in the country, but also in our heart geography. The Caucasus, particularly the Balkan countries, provides acreditation services for companies in various countries in Central Asia and North Africa, providing technical support for the country's standardisation and Akreditation institutions within the framework of co-operation. For example, Turkey supports efforts to strengthen its quality infrastructure in Mongolia, giving theoretical, practical training to Azerbaijan, Uzbekistan, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzistan, Moldova, Albania, Tunisia and Sudan. TÜRKAK, meanwhile, is conducting the accordion service, as well as monitoring developments in the world's economy, making preparations for further addition in new areas, such as greenhouse gases emissions, certified reference materials production, energy management systems fields. tbmm-2016-02-29sit03spe0178par0220	Mr. President and my valued deputies, the 19th article of the International Labour Organization (ILO) Convention (ILO) shall be submitted to the authorities for the purpose of informing members of the agreements and recommendations adopted at the International Work Conference. Mr. President, my esteemed lawmakers, I'd like to give you a brief statement on the International Labour Conference of the International Labour Organization 104 last June, and a recommendation for the Transgression from Foreign Economics, which was voted by our country at the end of the conference. The June 12th, 2015, and the Out-of-Register Economics Advice Decision for Transgression to the Registered Economy are mainly considered measures for facilitating the transition to the registered economy with the main problems of the informal economy. This recommendation, which has no bindings on member states, is more of a guide, includes guiding principles, legal and political frameworks, incentives and harmonisation of employment policies to facilitate transition to the registered economy, and consists of nine chapters and 42 articles total. The recommendation is recommended for member states, workers and economic units to move from off-the-book economies to registered economies, ensure income assurance and entrepreneurial opportunities under the basic rights of workers, the creation of initiative and respectable jobs in a registered economy, the creation of employment and social protection, and the harmonisation of other social policies. The recommendation is also noted for the necessity of employment policies that encourage employment, improve efficiency and enable structural transformation processes, and also for the removal of all forms of forced labour, including the rights of people working in the informal economy, to protect and carry out their rights, especially the freedom to organise and collective bargaining rights. In the process of transforming registered economies, measures such as gradually expanding the scope of national social protection, reducing registration costs and procedures in this context, improving simplified tax and premium assessment systems, facilitating costs, facilitating access to loan services that fit the scale and needs of economic units, entrepreneurial, education, skill and business development services are recommended. With respect to the knowledge of the High Council. tbmm-2016-02-29sit03spe0243par0511	On this occasion, I would like to share with you my assessment of the significant developments in Turkey-European Union relations. We are going through a very important period in terms of the future of the European project and Turkey-EU relations. We're going through an important period of change on the international platform that will be felt in our geography for a really long time. The process also put the European continent to severe trials. 2015 was a year when even the most fundamental policies of European integration became questionable. During this period, there was a major crisis in Greece, and the eurozone faced serious problems. After that, the European Union was forced to fight the largest influx of irregular migrations since World War II. In addition, terrorist attacks on the largest cities of Europe have left the European Union facing another critical problem. In addition, the debates of Britain's exit from the European Union added to all these problems, and the European Union began to live with existential crises. All these recent developments have led to questioning the idea of a multicultural European construction, based on peace and stability, rising on universal values. Turkey's importance for the future and stability of the Union has been more important than ever, as the European Union faces an irregular immigration crisis, a threat of terrorism and an economic crisis. Turkey was clearly one of the most indispensable actors to move forward the European project. More than ever, the European Union's closer approach to Turkey in the face of difficult trials will pass through European crises, but Europe will become Europe with solutions to these crises. When the word is remembered, it becomes more meaningful. As we've always stressed, Turkey will be one of the most fundamental factors to determine the future regional and global role and power of the European Union. Today's tests also proved the accuracy of this vision by bringing back common goals and common interests, which have been ignored for some time. Mr. President, the esteemed lawmakers are one of the most important actors in the history of radical democratic tradition, functioning market economy and geostrategic position in Turkey, in order for the European Union to revive its global and regional power and be effective in the face of current trials. At this point, Turkey's EU membership is being discussed on a strategic basis, and the basis of common interests is a genuinely intense and result-oriented effort. The paradigm in Turkey-EU relations has changed, so to speak, and the cards are being redistributed. As a matter of fact, we have recently found a new basis in our relations with the European Union that allows us to talk about common topics from the perspective of the future, not from the past. This ground also contains some elements in Turkey-EU relations that will really make your image feel stronger in the coming period and allow us to look more securely ahead. First, new paradigms and new elements that we have not seen in Turkey-EU relations in a really long time have come into play in the environment we've been talking about changing cards with the new paradigm. Turkey's European Union Summit, which our Prime Minister attended in November of 2015, was Turkey's first summit after 11 years. However, important decisions have been made along with this summit. The decision to make these summits twice a year in the future is not only the summits that have been shook hands and taken a family photo, but also the decisions that have been made, important instructions for the future, and that is how the Commission and its European Union structures will comply. Some of the most important issues in these areas have been created, and these platforms will carry all of these important issues to summit meetings. The first platform has been in the field of energy. The high-level energy co-operation platform has been determined to ensure safe access to the resources of natural gas in our region as well as to the joint movement of Turkey and the European Union in times of distress. And the first meeting was held on 28 January in Turkey, with the arrival of the Spanish Commissioner Canete, who is in charge of energy, and the participation of our Minister of Energy. The 2nd platform is in economics. Indeed, in the coming period between Turkey and the European Union's economies, a strong European Union, a strong region, and in which Turkey can play a role, how the business can play a role, as such as a mini Davos meeting, will be held in Istanbul in mid-April. And here, by participating with the six commissioners of the European Union and bringing business representatives from all countries of the European Union, from various parts of the Union, both the resources of our region and the resources of Turkey and the possibilities of the European Union will be tried to bring together. The third important issue is the customs union. Turkey has achieved significant achievements and achievements in the customs union relationship. Turkey is the only country to join the customs union without a member of the European Union, but Turkey, which is facing industrial giants and economic giants today, has in fact been able to restructure its industry and economy and to trade equally with these forces and to deal with a large number of goods. We're talking about 150 billion dollars in trade today, and hopefully with the updating of the customs union in this new order, we can achieve a $300 billion trade with the addition of agriculture, public purchases and services sector. Given that the trade between the United States and the European Union is $700 billion, it is clear how important it is to raise the trade of Turkey-European Union to $300 billion, in terms of the world, in terms of Europe, in terms of our region and Turkey. One of the key factors in the painting, which has emerged from gains and new paradigm changes, is that the visa will be lifted at the end of this year. Turkey has been forced to live with one of the bad heritages of the 1980 military coup that has been left to us for thirty-six years, and the citizens of the Republic of Turkey, hopefully with this new mechanism, will be able to enter countries in the Schengen area without visas, without even showing their passports. Another issue is the mechanism of political dialogue, which is one of the most important mechanisms. The contributions of the European Union and Turkey in all decisions are discussed at four-way meetings, at meetings attended by our Foreign Minister, and meetings consisting of the Minister of Foreign Affairs, the Minister of the EU, the High Representative and the Commissioner of Enlargement will be held with specific chapters, and this mechanism is a new platform in which we can deal with both the issues of our negotiation process and the fight against illegal immigration both with economic issues and with terrorism. Of course, in addition to all of this, there has been a revival of our long slow-going negotiation process. After a two-year break, we opened one chapter, the 17th Economy and Money Policys, last December. Hopefully, we're doing all the work this year to open up five more chapters, and we believe we're going to be successful in these matters. There's important stuff. In the 23rd chapter, political criteria are major, and it is a mechanism that our friends in the European Union, who have some opinions about Turkey and sometimes have perceptions of false information, are able to express this on official platforms that will come out with the opening of chapter 23 of the chapter 23 of the European Union, to criticize us, to apply these criticisms if we find them right, to share our views on these issues with the right information, and to provide them with insights on the other side. The 24th chapter... in an environment where we're dealing with illegal immigration, where we're talking about border security, about passport security, about re-accession agreements, where we're talking about integration management of borders, the 24th chapter, although it's not open to all these elements, is really ironic and unfair. Thus, we continue contact with the European Union with all the grounds for opening the 24th chapter before the resuscitation agreement is in effect. Education, energy and foreign policy and security chapters, I hope, will be enough to open this year. Naturally, a few more important issues in this relationship -- indeed, in our European Union relations, we've used 4.7 billion euros of grants so far, and the mechanisms that have come up with this have actually been very positive for our citizens, who, in a sense, created both projects in our country and in a sense, brought it to their cities, and hopefully, by 2020, we've been able to use a new IPA contribution of 4.5 billion euros. The framework agreement on this has been signed, and 4.5 billion euros will be used by 2020. The other important thing is Project Erasmus. Erasmus projects are a very common relationship that provides both our young people and, for example, an apprentice who works at an auto repair shop or a very important opportunity for our people at an older age. So far we've used 600m euros, 100,000 projects have been produced, and 26,000 have been accepted, 400,000 of our people have benefited, 300,000 have been able to go abroad. In the coming period, we have a new contribution of about 800m euros by 2020, and we are continuing all of our work in a way that, hopefully, 500,000 people can use. Project production is of great importance in both the use of IPA funds and the introduction of Erasmus projects. In order to achieve project production, we strive to bring project education to our country, both as the Ministry of the European Union and as the Development Agency, the Chamber Association, regional institutions, civil society together. Naturally, one of the most important issues in the coming period would be our co-operation in fighting this illegal migration. Indeed, an earthquake occurred, and we're dealing with one of the largest migrations in our history, perhaps under the tsunami after this earthquake.
